They hold the next game night at Lena's.

She invites Jack and Veronica, and while she has every right to—because it's her apartment, after all—Kara's a little disappointed. It means she'll pair up with Veronica, and as a result she can't be Kara's partner like she usually is. But it's fine, Kara reasons with herself. Jack's nice enough. And she's trying very hard not to be jealous of Veronica anymore.

It doesn't really work, since she lives with Lena now; Kara is confronted with the reality of Veronica's presence everywhere. If Lena isn't home, she's out with Veronica; if she stays up late on the phone, she's talking to Veronica; if she ducks out early in the morning, it's to see Veronica before work.

Part of Kara thinks it's sweet, if she's being perfectly honest. But a larger part of her is so jealous she doesn't know how she's going to handle it, and she hates that she can't be unequivocally happy for her best friend.

And it doesn't help that Lena's the most thoughtful person ever. Kara can hear her now, on the phone with Veronica: "No, that's not the kind Kara likes—I said cheese, yes. Hold—hold on a second. Winn, what are you feeding Mike?"

"Mashed potatoes! They're very soft, don't worry."

Kara gives James's shoulder an absentminded pat as she takes that as her cue to go; she enters the kitchen just in time to catch Winn give Mike another taste off his plate.

"He just ate, Winn," Kara chides, and she plucks Mike up out of Winn's lap when he raises his arms for her. "Come here little boy. Stop charming people out of their food.”

Mike welcomes the attention, giddily mumbling gibberish as Kara takes him back into the living room. Kara pauses by the counter so she can feed their goldfish, the not-very-rightly named Nemo. (Carter picked it out.)

“Alright, everyone, I have the games,” Winn announces, emerging from the kitchen with a Monopoly board over his head.

Alex immediately shuts him down. “No.”

“What?”

“No, we’re not playing Monopoly. Put it back,” she demands. “I’m not trying to stay here all night.”

“Plus, Maggie cheats,” Kara’s sure to add, which Alex gravely nods in agreement with.

Maggie pauses the movie on TV just to crane her neck backwards, betrayed. “I don’t cheat,” she says. “It’s all strategy.”

“Your strategy is stealing from the bank, Sawyer. It’s cheating,” Alex says.

“You’re accusing a police officer of stealing? That’s a new low, Danvers.”

Kara tunes out their bickering and claims her previous spot on the couch, right against James’s side. Mike squirms to be set down, so she allows him to play in his baby gym instead of bringing him with her.

He’s close enough that she can watch him anyway, and still be able to rest her chin on James’s shoulder so she can peek at his phone. “What are you up to?” she asks. “Are you ordering pizza?”

“No, so don’t get any ideas. Lena’s already prepared enough food for an army.”

“Yeah, but it’s,” and here Kara drops her voice for dramatic effect, “healthy food.”

James snorts. “Like you wouldn’t eat all the kale in the world for her,” he ribs good-naturedly, which is a very low blow. “I’m sure you’ll survive.”

“Now that’s just uncalled for.”

“Okay, okay, I’ll drop it.” James brings up a page on his phone in a wordless subject change, holding it higher so Kara can see it. “What do you think about a themed photoshoot for Mike? Sailor outfits are all the rage.”

“James...that’s so ugly. He can’t wear that.”

“He’s wearing a onesie decorated with tacos right now, Kara. I think this would be a step up.”

“Are you questioning my taste in baby clothing?” Kara demands, feigning indignation on top of it. “Tacos are cute!”

James makes a noncommittal noise that Kara narrows her eyes at. Before she can annoy him about it further, there’s a knock at the door. Lena beats everyone to it, distractedly mumbling something into the phone balanced between her cheek and her shoulder.

It’s Veronica. She greets Lena with a chaste kiss as Lena helps lighten the load of grocery bags she’s holding, but otherwise, doesn’t look too pleased to be here. Kara has to look away, intently refocusing on James’s blank screen, and James gives her a sympathetic pat to the head.

“It must suck,” he says, “to have to see Veronica more.”

Kara bites the inside of her cheek and mumbles, “Feelings are stupid,” against his shirt. She gets another pat on the head, and she has to drop it before she starts getting jealous again. “Distract me. Tell me about your wedding plans.”

James is only too happy to oblige. “Well,” he says, “we were thinking a summer wedding…”

Unsurprisingly, the evening becomes more bearable once Kara makes herself stop thinking about Veronica and Lena. Winn squeezes between them on the couch a while later and declares he will not, under any circumstances, have any other ring bearer beside Mike.

“He might not be walking by then, Winn,” Kara says. “And he’d probably try to eat the rings.”

“Huh. Didn’t think of that,” Winn says, frowning. “What if we make him the flower boy?”

“He would try to eat the flower petals.”

“Okay, so we push the wedding—”

“I feel like that should be a decision you make with me first,” James points out, large smile betraying any seriousness in his voice.

“Oh, I’m sorry, where are you involved in all this?” Winn feigns confusion, but in a second he’s grinning right back and tugging at the end of James’s tie to kiss him.

Kara lets them have their moment. She takes a seat on the floor beside Mike, who has given up playing and is instead sucking on his hand and staring up at the toys hanging over his head.

“Hey,” Kara whispers, shaking one of them to catch his attention as she pulls his fist out of his mouth. “No trying to eat your own hand, you.”

He smiles, and makes a small sound that sounds like AH. Kara lifts him up into her arms and kisses his cheek because of it; he’s not a very talkative baby, but he has his moments. And it’s the cutest every single time.

“Kara? Kara, we need you,” Winn calls, dragging her out of the moment. “Lena doesn’t know how to work her own blender.”

“It’s Kara’s blender, it’s not mine,” comes Lena’s faint argument. “I don’t own a blender.”

“How do you not own a blender?”

Kara carries Mike with her into the kitchen, where Winn and Lena seem to be arguing over how an existence without a blender is absolutely plausible (Lena’s argument) or how such an existence is pointless (Winn’s argument). Mike has no idea what they’re talking about, but he yells excitedly in tandem; he is mostly yelling at Lena, probably because he is over the moon to see her.

Lena is more than happy to take Mike out of Kara’s arms and leave them to the issue of the blender. Winn mumbles something about “a renounced genius falling at the hands of a blender” and Kara subtly elbows him for it—even as he grasps into his side, betrayed—because it makes Lena laugh on her way out.

“Sooo,” Winn says afterwards, sliding up beside Kara to mock-whisper. “I have an idea. We can sous out whether or not Lena’s really into Veronica.”

“Winn, no.”

“Hear me out—truth or dare. High school style.”

“No, Winn,” Kara stresses. “If they’re dating it’s pretty obvious she has to like Veronica.”

“But how much is the question,” Winn argues as they dump ingredients for milkshakes into the blender jar. “I’ll bet she just likes the idea of Veronica. You know, that’s how it goes in all the romance novels! Nostalgia and all that.”

“This isn’t a romance novel, this is real life,” Kara says. “Hey. Where’d you get marshmallows? Will those even blend?”

The marshmallows do not blend, and as a result, they eat ice cream straight out of the tub as they play pictionary. Even if it’s vegan ice cream, Lena’s stocked about ten different flavors just to be prepared for game night; it helps that everyone gets their own tub, even if Kara’s the only one really dedicated to making a proper dent in it.

Mike dozes off against her chest halfway through, having had too much excitement for the day. He’s been very finicky around anyone beside Kara—or Lena—as of late. It makes sense, if they’re the only two people he sees consistently. It makes Kara start to think about Adam, and how he would feel if he were here too. Maybe he wouldn’t even notice. Lately he seems like he’s off in his own world.

“Kara, what do you think?”

Kara starts. “What?”

“Of Veronica’s drawing,” Lena says. She has a question in her eyes she doesn’t voice, and she rests her hand on Mike’s back soothingly even if he’s not awake to notice. “Jack thinks it’s a bear. I’m pretty sure it’s a car.”

“Sure, a car sounds right,” Kara says. Privately she thinks it looks more like a walrus, but she doesn’t say so.

“Final answer,” Lena says. “It’s a car.”

Veronica sends her an absolutely unimpressed look. “It’s a dog,” she says, which makes Winn and James shout and hi-five.

“My turn,” Alex groans, taking a healthy swig of her beer before she actually stands.

“Don’t blow our lead, babe!” Maggie shouts. To James and Winn she says, “We’re screwed,” which of course makes Alex flip her off.

“We’re just getting warmed up!” Jack shouts, and while everyone else engages in playful trash talk Kara excuses herself to set Mike to bed.

She doesn’t realize Lena has followed her until she feels her hand gently touch her elbow. “Hey,” Lena says. “You okay?”

“Yes...do I not seem like it, or something?”

“Not exactly,” Lena says, but she’s pursing her lips apologetically like she knows she’s lying. “But kind of? I’m sorry if this isn’t what you had in mind. I know I haven’t been to many of your game nights, so…”

“It’s perfect, hey,” Kara says. “Don’t worry about it.” She kisses Mike’s head as she sets him down, tucking his blanket snugly around his body. “It’s going great.”

“We’ve been here before,” Lena says, leaning against the wall with a quiet laugh. “Is it weird? All we’re really doing is having our friends meet but I haven’t felt this nervous since I introduced my mother to a girl for the first time.”

“That sounds like a fun story,” Kara offers, promptly mirroring Lena’s stance.

“It was a nightmare. I threw up halfway through brunch.”

“At least this time we can be sure no one will puke,” Kara says. “Mike’s very good at keeping his food down now, and he’s out for the count.”

Lena rests her head back and sighs. “You must think I’m ridiculous,” she says. “I feel ridiculous. It’s not a big deal to have everyone over like this.”

“Are you kidding? It is a big deal,” Kara says. “We’re becoming one crazy, happy family, just like we were always meant to be.”

That gets her a small smile. “Always, huh?”

“Absolutely,” Kara says. “You’re not about to get rid of us that easy.”

Lena gives her a genuine, joyful grin, and they stay like that for a little while and pretend they don’t hear Winn calling for them to return.

(Plus, Lena elaborates on the story with her mother and her ex, which makes it much easier for Kara to forget about everything else but this moment.)

.

.

.

The apartment feels too noisy now.

Kara tosses and turns and gets up at least twice, just to check on Mike as he sleeps in his crib. It’s just the nerves, she knows, but she’s just not ready for any change; from her spot on the bed she can see the crib tucked against the wall, standing taller than his bassinet had. If she concentrates hard enough she can hear his breathing, low and steady, and it should be soothing but it isn’t.

Every sound from outside makes her skin prickle. A car passes; a branch scrapes a window; sirens wail somewhere in the distance. Kara listens to every one and then panics, because that means she isn’t listening to Mike.

The only solution she comes up with to this problem is to sit up in bed and scroll through news feeds on her phone; at least that way she has a chance of staying awake. On impulse she sends Lena a text that reads II can't sleep :(and doesn’t think much of it, already pulling up the latest CatCo article that she’d usually save to read over breakfast.

(She certainly doesn’t expect Lena to start calling her.)

“Hello?”

“Hey.” Lena’s voice is barely a whisper. “You should be asleep.”

“You should be too,” Kara says. A beat. “You know, I’m literally two doors down from you.”

“And your point is?” It sounds like Lena’s smiling. “Talk to me. Why can’t you sleep?”

“I don’t know. I’m just...I’m thinking about Mike.” Kara feels silly admitting it aloud, but Lena just waits for her to continue. “And how he’s sleeping in that crib for the first time. What if we didn’t build it right? What if it’s uncomfortable?”

“Does he look like he’s uncomfortable?”

“He’s asleep.” Kara rests her head against the wall, shoulders slumping as a surge of exhaustion overcomes her. “And I feel like I’m supposed to stay awake and watch him. To make sure he’s okay. Which probably sounds dumb.”

“It’s not dumb. I get it.”

“Yeah?”

“Yeah.” After this Lena’s line goes quiet, but it doesn’t sound like she’s fallen asleep. “Would you feel better if you had some company?”

Kara tries—and fails—to keep her heart from thumping dangerously quick. “Like a sleepover?” she says weakly.

There is a pregnant pause, again. “I guess so,” Lena says faintly. “Would that be okay?”

“It would.”

The click of the call ending only makes Kara’s heart go crazier. In a minute Lena is quietly pushing open her door, dressed in the fuzzy pajamas Kara had bought her on an impulse, running her fingers through her messy hair. She might smile, Kara thinks, but it’s hard to tell in the dark.

“I can sleep on the floor if you want,” Lena says softly.

“No, don’t,” Kara blurts out, a bit louder than intended. “I—I mean, if you’re okay with it, you can stay up here with me.”

“Okay.” Lena awkwardly comes to rest on the edge of the bed, and once Kara scoots over, gets under the covers beside her. “If you’re sure you don’t mind?”

“Of course I don’t mind. You’re my best friend,” Kara says, nudging Lena’s arm with her elbow. Lena stiffens when Kara edges closer to her, which only makes Kara’s heart sink. “I’m sorry. Is it weird to sleep with you?”

“Um.” Lena shifts a little. Maybe she’s restless. “No? I’m just not used to sleeping beside anyone.”

“As long as you don’t snore I won’t mind,” Kara teases, and that works to make Lena laugh. Well, it’s more like a huff of a laugh, but it’s a laugh nonetheless.

“I don’t snore.”

“Uh-huh,” Kara says. “I’ll believe it when I see it.” She wishes she could see Lena’s face, to better understand what she’s thinking. “Lena? Can I hold you?”

There is a long moment of silence. “What?”

Now it’s a good thing they’re not facing each other, because Kara feels her face burn hot. It was a stupid thing to say to begin with, she knows; they’re friends, and she’s not trying to push outside of any boundary that automatically sets for their relationship, but—it’s not like they don’t touch each other. That’s one of their things, the casual touches that are comfortable. Easy.

Lena is never a really touchy person with anyone else, she knows, and maybe this is new to her; Kara doesn’t really want to come out and say I like knowing you're being held by someone who cares about you, and I know you like that feeling too.

Her disastrous brain, however, does manage to get her to blurt out, “I know how much you like being touched.”

“...what?”

“Not like—I just want to cuddle you! That’s it!”

In a second Lena’s hand is being placed over her mouth. “Shh,” she warns. “You’re about to yell.”

“Oh. Shoot. I wasn’t thinking,” Kara says, and the reality that her mouth is muffled by Lena’s hand means her words come out garbled.

Lena slowly lowers her hand, and Kara is captivated. There’s no other word for it. Her fingers drag against Kara’s lips and those green eyes meet her hers in the dark and it feels—it feels so heavy. Their faces are so close that she can imagine what it would be like to lean in and bridge that small gap and it is...really very distracting.

Finally Lena speaks, and it’s with a cautious smile that she says, “So is cuddling part of the sleepover experience?”

Kara clears her throat, and tries to laugh. “Obviously,” she says. “That’s why I offered.”

“Mm. Well, I wouldn’t know,” Lena says. “I’ve never had a proper sleepover before. I tried to make Lex have a sleepover with me once, but he always insisted my dolls were haunted and wouldn’t set foot in my room. I hold it over his head to this day.”

“Every time you talk about Lex I feel like I should be taking notes for blackmail material.”

“You don’t have to worry about that, I blackmail him with it already,” Lena rolls back over on her side so they’re no longer face-to-face, but it’s with a relaxed sigh; Lex isn’t a touchy subject tonight, it seems.

Kara takes that moment to tentatively place her arm over Lena’s waist, which is both a wonderful and torturous feeling. She takes pride in the fact that Lena sinks into the touch, and nestles even closer, until she can feel the tickle of Lena’s hair against her face.

“Thank you for staying,” Kara feels compelled to whisper, because she really doesn’t want them to stay in silence. “I’m really glad you’re my friend.”

“Yeah, okay, you sap,” Lena says, but it sounds like she’s smiling, and she’s begun to trace patterns against the back of Kara’s hand lightly. “I guess I like you too.”

“Don’t act like you don’t love me. I’m the best roommate you’ve ever had.”

“You’re the only roommate I’ve ever had. Unless Mike counts. Then in that case I like him better.”

“Everyone picks him over me. It’s so mean,” Kara says. “I do see the appeal, though. He’s hands down my favorite son.”

Lena snorts at that. “He’s your only son,” she says, and then decides, “You’re so ridiculous. I think you need to get some sleep.”

“I can’t go to sleep. That defeats the purpose of a sleepover,” Kara says. “And I’m still worried about Mike.”

“You can sleep for a little while. I’ll stay awake and make sure Mike’s okay,” Lena says. “Then I’ll wake you up so we can trade.”

“You can’t do that, you need sleep more than I do!” Kara has to pause for a second, there. “You really don’t sleep enough to begin with.”

“Kara. If you’re staying up then so I am. You can’t change my mind.”

“You’re a very stubborn person, do you know that?” Kara says, pretending to be miffed, but she gratefully squeezes Lena’s waist and feels the way her body shakes with quiet laughter.

“Takes one to know one,” she says. “So. Tell me what else we’re supposed to do in a sleepover. The movies always say ghost stories, and I have a really good one.”

Kara buries her face against Lena’s shoulder, feeling warm and comfortable and, oddly enough, at ease. Lena tends to have that effect. “Okay,” she says, smiling. “Hit me.”

.

.

.

Marked under one of the oddest work days of all time? This one. And it’s not even one o’clock yet.

Cat is in a strange mood. She’s cancelled two meetings just so she can talk on the phone, and won’t let anyone within three feet of her office to check up on her. It’s cryptic even for her, and Kara isn’t sure what this means; the last time Cat was in this state was when Adam first came to town, and that had been a rough week for everyone involved.

When her break for lunch rolls around she calls Alex, to see what she thinks about all this. Alex thinks it’s not that big a deal.

“But I’ve never seen Ms. Grant like this,” Kara says. “It’s strange. She’s not distressed or anything, but she’s...I don’t know. She seems like she has a lot on her mind.”

“She’s your boss, Kara. I don’t think she pays you to worry about her state of mind,” says Alex, likely around a mouthful of food; she must be taking her lunch at the same time.

“That’s so mean, Alex!” Kara says. “She’s not just my boss. We’re practically family now.”

“Because she’s Mike’s grandma?”

“Don’t call her that, Winn swears these lines are bugged.”

Alex is probably rolling her eyes right about now. “I’m sure Cat will let you know what’s going on eventually,” she says. “Or if not knowing is really bugging you that much, you could just call Adam and see if he has any inside info.”

“That would be weird,” Kara says. “We’re not...you know. We’re not in the best place.”

“Oh. I’m sorry,” Alex says. “I thought you two were friends again.”

Kara sighs. “We’re trying to be. But it’s hard to go back to the way things were,” she says. “Maybe it was a mistake to move out.” She unpacks her lunch as she speaks, just to busy her hands with something. She finds that she’s lost her appetite already.

“I would’ve thought you were thrilled you moved out,” Alex says, “since you moved in with Lena. How’s that going? Maggie’s convinced you’re dying.”

“It’s going just fine,” Kara says, defensively, and she doesn’t even realize she’s gripped her plastic fork too hard until she feels it crack. “Nothing’s really changed. With us, at least. I just...see her a lot more. And once we slept together, but it wasn’t really a big deal because she left before I woke up. And one time I came home just as Veronica was leaving, which wasn’t fun, but—”

“Uh. Can you backtrack for a second, there? What do you mean you slept together?”

“We had a sleepover,” Kara says. “She was helping me with Mike.”

“A sleepover. Okay. Please be more specific next time,” Alex coughs.

“What?”

“Nothing. Go on. Living with Lena is terrible because…”

“It isn’t! That’s the problem,” Kara says. “All it’s doing is making me see how much I like her. Gosh, Alex, I like her so much. And I’m the world’s worst best friend for thinking that.”

“It’s not a bad thing to have a crush, Kara,” Alex tells her patiently. “She’d be so lucky to date you.”

“You’re supposed to say that, you’re my sister.” Glumly, Kara stabs a piece of broccoli and eyes it suspiciously, because this is decidedly not the lasagna she’d packed last night. Sure enough, when she checks her messages she finds she has one waiting from Lena.

It reads I think I took your lunch by accident? and it really shouldn’t make her smile as much as she does.

Alex’s voice brings her back to reality soon enough. “Why don’t you try dating again?”

“What? No. I did, remember?” Kara says, too concerned with tapping out a reply to Lena assuring her it’s okay (even if Kara might not make it through this meal). “That didn’t work out at all.”

“Maybe Jack just wasn’t your type,” Alex says. “Maggie says they got a new transfer to the precinct a few days ago. She’s been bugging me to give you her number, if you’re interested.”

“I am very not interested in dating again, thank you,” Kara says.

At that exact moment she looks away from her lunch, about to cast a glance in Winn’s direction to mouth for him to save her, when she notices that she’s not alone. Even worse, she might not have been alone for a while.

Siobhan Smythe is standing in front of her desk. This might be a hallucination.

“You say that now, but you haven’t even met this new girl,” Alex goes on, entirely unaware that Kara is about to die on the spot. “I’ll give her your number.”

Siobhan smiles when she sees that Kara’s finally noticed her, but it’s not really a smile so much as it is an amused twist of the left corner of her mouth. Either way, it makes Kara freeze.

“Uh,” she says, “I’m going to have to call you back?” She doesn’t even wait for Alex to respond before she slams her work phone back into its dock, an awkward laugh escaping before she can stop it. “Hi. Um. Siobhan? What—what are you doing here?”

“Hi,” Siobhan says, smile growing wider. “What a small world.” She holds out a portfolio, which Kara—in her confusion—takes, and continues, “So you must be Ms. Grant’s assistant.”

“I am,” Kara says. “I’m sorry, what exactly can I do for you?”

“I have an appointment,” Siobhan says. “Well, more like an interview. At one-thirty?”

“Huh. Well, I didn’t…” Before Kara can apologize and say there’s no interview on the schedule, she finds that indeed there is one; it’s clearly been added in by Ms. Grant, because she hasn’t touched the schedule since yesterday. “Oh. Let me check with Ms. Grant, and see if she’s still available. I thought I’d cleared the rest of her afternoon.”

“I can reschedule, if—”

“No, no, just give me a minute?” Kara feels a tad flustered as she rises, flashing an apologetic smile before she scrambles to Cat’s office. She catches Winn’s eyes as she rises, and he’s gesturing in a very frantic and not-at-all subtle way—probably to ask why Siobhan’s here—and the most she can give him is a shrug.

Cat calls for her to come in when she knocks. She is furiously texting when Kara shuts the door behind her, and only spares her a, “What happened this time, Kiera?”

“Um, I didn’t cancel your one-thirty,” Kara says. “Should I tell Siobhan to come back later, or—?”

“Who?”

“Siobhan Smythe,” Kara says patiently. “She’s set for an interview at one-thirty.”

That elicits a curious reaction; Cat almost perks up. “Oh, that’s right,” she says. “She’s here for the assistant position.”

“The…” Kara stops. “You mean my job?”

Cat sighs, shaking her head disapprovingly at the way Kara gapes. “Don’t act so surprised,” she sniffs. “You and I both knew this was only temporary.” She beckons for Kara to come closer, and Kara—in somewhat of a daze—does.

Kara sits down across from Cat and places her hands in her lap, not sure if she’s allowed to ask questions when she’s getting fired. Is she allowed to be fired before the day’s even over? Is she supposed to have rights for this kind of thing?

“Don’t let it get to your head,” Cat says, either unaware of Kara’s panic or ignoring it, “but being my assistant has always been a waste of your potential.”

“But I’m great at it!” Kara blurts out. “I’m sorry Ms. Grant. I didn’t mean to interrupt. B-but I’ve always been a dedicated worker, and I really need this job—”

“I’m not firing you, Kiera,” Cat cuts her off, giving her an exasperated look like she can’t believe Kara is even suggesting it. “I’m promoting you.”

And that makes Kara relax, a little. But also not. “Oh,” she says dumbly. “What am I being promoted to?”

“That’s up to you to decide.” Cat leans back into her chair, casting a scrutinizing eye on the way Kara fidgets. “You wrote up decent notes when you surveyed that law firm...Zorel something or other. You wouldn’t be half bad as a writer.”

“Really?” Kara perks up.

“Or whatever it is you choose,” Cat says. “I’m prepared to offer you any position we have here. Take some time to figure it out.”

“That’s,” Kara shakes her head, “I—I don’t even know what to say, Ms. Grant. Thank you.”

“Don’t thank me just yet,” Cat says. “I’m counting on you not to let me down with your choice. Now,” and here, she nods towards the door, “bring in whoever’s waiting. Let’s hope I’m not letting you go for nothing, Kara.”

“You got it,” Kara says, getting to her feet. She knows Cat trusts her, but this—this feels now like Cat appreciates her. It’s the last thing she expected, but her first impression is that she doesn’t mind.

Though she won’t lie; some part of her is a little terrified, and maybe that shows on her face, because when she heads back to her desk Siobhan sees her and asks:

“Bad news?”

Kara blinks. For a moment she’d forgotten about Siobhan. “No,” she says. “Ms. Grant said she can do the interview. Um. I’m sorry I wasn’t aware of it—”

“That’s okay.” Siobhan crosses her arms, and tilts her head as if she wants to say something else. After a beat, she does. “So...this interview is for an assistant position. Ms. Grant must be overworking you if she needs two assistants.”

“Well, you’re actually interviewing for my job,” Kara says. “Not that I’d put it past Ms. Grant to actually hire two assistants.”

“Now that’s a shame,” Siobhan says, a curious smile forming on her lips. “And here I was looking forward to the possibility of us working closely together.” She places her hand, suddenly, on Kara’s forearm; her hand warmly slides to her elbow, causing goosebumps to form on Kara’s skin. “Unless you’re staying at CatCo?”

“I, uh,” Kara clears her throat, feels herself blush. “I am.”

“Then there’s hope yet,” Siobhan says. She looks past Kara’s head and adds, “I’d better not keep her waiting. I feel like that won’t make a good first impression.”

She might wink as she brushes past. Kara honestly can’t remember.

Winn slides up to her desk the instant the coast is clear, and says, “Kara, if you can’t tell that girl’s flirting with you now I feel like you’re beyond all hope.”

“...shut up, Winn.”

.

.

.

When Kara comes home, she hears running water and Mike’s laughter; it brightens her day in an instant, and for a moment, she forgets about the pressure to find a new job and Siobhan’s confusing flirting and just about everything else.

“Hey, you’re home,” Lena says. She’s sitting on the couch, absentmindedly chewing on the end of her pen as she flips through a stack of papers. “I told Maggie she could leave if she wanted, but she said she’d keep watching Mike until you came back. I hope that’s okay?”

“Of course,” Kara says, dropping her bag onto the coffee table. “How come you’re home early?”

“Jess kicked me out of the office,” Lena says, without looking up. She’s likely been home for a while; she’s wearing sweatpants and her reading glasses, the frames of which rest low on her nose. “So I had to take work here. Um, Maggie is giving Mike a bath?”

“That’s nice of her,” Kara says. “I meant to shower him when I got home anyway.”

“She is doing this in the sink, though?”

“Yeah, she does that,” Kara says. “I’ll relieve her of her babysitting duties for the night. Have you eaten yet? You can actually come home early and relax sometimes. That’s allowed.”

“Give me a minute and I’m all yours,” Lena promises, still without glancing at Kara once, but it makes Kara smile nonetheless.

She heads into the kitchen to meet Maggie—who is indeed showering Mike in their kitchen sink—and blows her baby a kiss when he notices her.

“Hey, Maggie, I’m home,” Kara says. “How was he?”

“He wasn’t that much trouble,” Maggie says, lifting him up high; he giggles, and looks back at Kara, practically twisting out of Maggie’s arms in his attempt to get his mom to hold him instead. “Hey, kid, don’t do that—if I drop you it’s on you.”

“Don’t tell him that,” Kara says, accepting her squirming bundle of a son once Maggie wraps him up into a towel. “I’m really sorry I dragged you away from your day off.”

“Come on, you know I’m always happy to see this little boy,” Maggie says, wiping her wet hands off onto her already soaked T-shirt. “How come Adam couldn’t make it? Isn’t he taking time off work to watch him?”

“He’s more or less gone back to work,” Kara says. “I don’t know. He works less days, but he’s still there.”

“That sucks.” Maggie wrings out the bottom of her shirt over the sink, definitely more preoccupied with that instead of Adam.

“Yeah.” Kara doesn’t want to think about Adam either, so she says, “Do you want to stay for dinner? Lena and I will probably order in.”

“No, that’s alright, Alex’s still at work,” Maggie says. “If I head back now I can cook something.”

“Okay. I’ll go get this troublemaker into some clothes, then,” Kara says. “Say bye to Maggie, Mike!”

Mike follows Maggie with wide eyes as she leans in to kiss his forehead, trying to crane his neck up when she gets too close, and squealing with her kiss takes him by surprise. Kara takes his fist and moves it like he’s waving goodbye, which he loves; he gets very excited when she waves back, and smacks his hand against his towel after Kara lets go.

“Bye, Lena,” Maggie calls as she’s halfway out the door, and Lena only pauses to lift her head to echo the goodbye; Kara can’t resist teasing,

“So I take it you’re not done?”

Lena finally looks at her, and she smiles faintly, almost like she can’t resist it. “Give me one more minute,” she says. “Promise.”

“I think I could take over Jess’s job,” Kara says. “It seems like all she does is try to get you to stop working so much.”

“Ha-ha,” Lena says, with a roll of her eyes that’s more amused than exasperated. “Mike, please don’t copy your mom’s sense of humor.”

“That’s rude,” Kara says. “Isn’t it, Mike?”

“Ah,” he babbles, and that brings about an unexpected burst of laughter from Lena.

“The both of you are going to gang up on me now, aren’t you?” she says. “Go away, you’re distracting me. Seriously, I’ll be done in a minute.”

“Okay,” Kara warns, “but when I come back from changing Mike you have to officially put away all work! We’re ordering Italian.”

“I know you just mean pizza, Kara. You don’t have to pretend it’s anything else,” Lena calls after her.

“Pizza is Italian! It sounds fancier that way,” Kara calls back, already halfway down the hallway.

“Somehow I think Domino’s is not what the Italians had in mind!”

Kara takes the warm feeling of this domesticity with her as she and Mike tackle their post-bath routine. He squirms away from her hands when she puts baby lotion on him; she kisses his stomach to make him laugh; he settles his head against the crook of her shoulder after he’s dressed in warm pajamas, damp hair tickling at the side of her neck.

These moments always give her some time to think. Having Mike tucked into her arms is so comforting that she feels like she could take on anything, if she wanted to.

“I know you’re too young to know what I’m asking you, Mike,” Kara whispers against his temple. “I don’t even know why I’m asking you. Because I’m not even sure if I’m ready, you know? Or if this is the best idea.” She strokes over his back with one hand, and sighs. “But what if your Aunt Alex is right? What if I need to properly start dating? That’s a dumb question, because you don’t know what dating is.” She pauses. “Also, don’t say dumb. That’s a bad word.”

He lifts his head up from her shoulder, apparently having decided he’s not in the mood to go to sleep. Kara tries to ease him back anyway, singing lullabies and rocking him back and forth, but he won’t have it; he starts to whine, and only lets up when she takes him back outside.

“Mike won’t sleep,” Kara announces, but she stops short when she realizes that Lena isn’t on the couch anymore.

She isn’t left wondering what’s happened, however, because a second later Lena emerges from her room out of breath.

“Kara, I’m sorry,” she says. “I forgot I was supposed to meet Veronica for dinner tonight. It completely slipped my mind, I don’t understand…” She trails off like she’s still a bit lost, gathering her papers off the coffee table to straighten up the living room.

She’s gotten changed out of her casual clothes but walks around frazzled, dress half-slipping off her shoulder because she hasn’t zipped it up yet. Her hair’s been thrown into a ponytail and her makeup is done but she is also still wearing her glasses; she just about trips over her own heels in her haste to get everything in order.

“It’s okay,” Kara says, quickly strapping Mike in to his baby swing so she can help. “Let me clean up, you can finish getting ready.”

“No, I—I should cancel,” Lena says. “Wait. I should’ve done that before I got…” She stops, suddenly, and flushes pink as her dress almost falls entirely off her torso. “Would you zip me up?”

“Yeah, come here,” Kara chuckles, easily sweeping Lena’s hair aside to do so. She’s not sure if her fingers shake as she finds the zipper, but she does almost forget how to breathe; suddenly she knows what her best friend’s naked back looks like. Which. Is not something she’s going to be able to get out of her mind for days, she’s sure.

“Thank you,” Lena sighs. “I’m such a mess tonight.” She bends over to strap on her heels, nearly falling over in the process. “I’ve given up, Veronica’s seen me looking worse.” She accidentally grabs Kara’s bag, fixes it with an odd look, and then exchanges it for hers immediately after. “I ordered your pizza, it should be arriving in fifteen minutes. Am I wearing lipstick? No, I remember putting it on. I think I’m wearing lipstick.”

“Lena.”

“Shit, she’s outside. I think I’m decent. Bye, Mike.” This she punctuates with a kiss to his head. “Bye, Kara.”

“Lena.”

“What? What am I missing?” Lena stops at the doorway, brow crinkled adorably in confusion.

Kara reaches out and gently slides Lena’s reading glasses off her face, just slow enough that she sees every flutter of eyelashes when Lena blinks, just lightly enough that Lena can’t tell how much she almost second-guesses herself. “Have fun, okay?” she says. “We’ll have Italian some other time.”

“It’s still just pizza,” Lena says, but she has an earnest smile on her face, and—in an unexpected move—she throws her arms around Kara’s neck.

Kara hugs her as tightly as she dares, stomach flip-flopping all the while, but it’s still over too soon. Unlike any other night where Lena goes to meet Veronica, Kara doesn’t feel a rush of jealousy simmering; no, it’s something much more manageable, but just barely so.

It’s the kind of feeling that makes her think maybe she can get over Lena if she tries. How hard can it possibly be?

12. i can't remember to forget you

Notes for the Chapter:

i have no excuses i'm so sorry

It’s early one morning, when there’s toothpaste on her tongue and Mike is gloomily chewing on a teething ring, that Kara remembers she doesn’t have a babysitter.

Ten minutes later she is saying, “Adam, you said you didn’t have to work,” about as patient as anyone awake at 5 in the morning can be. “Eliza can’t watch Mike today.”

“I know, I know, I’m sorry. I’ll see if I can set up a sitter. Just give me an hour, okay? An hour.”

“Okay,” Kara relents. “Let me know as soon as you do.”

Adam continues to apologize profusely nonetheless, and Kara’s not rude enough to hang up, but she gives Lena a universal can you believe this? gesture towards her phone as she joins her in the kitchen.

Lena only smiles apologetically back. Usually she’s up and out too early for breakfast, but curiously enough she’s taking her time this morning; there’s a mug of coffee waiting for Kara, and Lena herself is sitting at the table and sipping at her own like she has all the time in the world.

“No, really—it’s okay. I wouldn’t say it was okay if it wasn’t, I promise,” Kara sighs. “You don’t owe me anything. It’s your day with Mike. As long as you take care of a sitter then it’s fine…”

Lena raises her eyebrows at that. Kara purses her lips and mouths I know.

“Great. Yeah. Just, keep me updated please,” Kara says. “Okay. I have—no, that’s fine—I have to go.” She shifts Mike on her hip, annoyed, and feels him start to lean out of her arms and stretch his hands out towards Lena instead. “Mike, hey! Don’t—yes, Adam, I am busy. So I have to go. Okay. That’s okay. Great, bye!”

She secures Mike in her arms the instant she tosses her phone to the side, giving her son a very firm admonishing look. He doesn’t even bother trying to meet her eyes, still squirming in Lena’s direction and babbling insistently as if he’s trying to alert her of his distress.

Kara can’t help but soften. “Mike, baby, what’s wrong?” she says. “Why are you so restless this morning, huh?”

“Here, let me hold him,” Lena says, already setting her coffee aside to stand. It’s said so flippantly that for a second Kara just gapes; this might be the first time Lena has offered to do so without any pressing reason to, and with no hesitation at all on her part.

“Are you sure?” Kara says, but Lena is already brushing up beside her and Mike is happily falling into her open arms.

Lena sits back down, allowing Mike to engage in the happy task in trying to play with her breakfast. “What’s happening with Adam?” she asks. “Is everything okay?”

“Yes and no,” Kara admits, falling into the chair on the opposite end of the table so she can inhale her own mug of coffee. “He can’t take care of Mike today and Eliza’s out of town, so I don’t have a babysitter.”

“Well…I could watch him.”

Kara stops her mug halfway to her lips. “What?”

“I can watch him,” Lena repeats. “I mean, my brother’s the boss, so it’s not like anyone can tell me I can’t bring Mike to work with me.”

“But you…you can’t be babysitting at work,” Kara says. “You’re really busy!”

“I’ll have an easy day today,” Lena swears. “Really, I won’t spend a moment without him by my side. Or Jess’s, if there’s an emergency. But I don’t think there will be.”

“Lena—”

“Kara,” Lena says. “I’m not going to give up until you say yes.” She lifts Mike so he’s standing on her legs, and he grins sunnily up at Kara. “Mike agrees with me, don’t you Mike?”

“Eugg.”

“Are you using my son against me? That is very unfair,” Kara says. She feels her smile even before it forms, and the triumphant way Lena bites her lip should not be as attractive as it is. “Are you sure it’s okay?”

“Of course it is,” Lena says. “I want to help you.” She always says such earnest things that make it harder and harder to remember Kara should be trying to get over her. It’s really very inconvenient. “Besides, I could use a cute face to win over the board.”

Kara can’t even try to keep her fond smile at bay. “I owe you one,” she says. “Thank you, Lena. I’ll text Adam right now and let him know.”

“It’s the least I can do.” There’s a quiet, curious edge to the way Lena smiles back, but if she has something else to add she doesn’t voice it.

“I will definitely come by for lunch,” Kara’s sure to add, jumping to her feet to find some sugar for her cup. “I’ll have Eve handle Siobhan’s training.”

“Oh, Siobhan.” Lena raises an eyebrow. “Alex tells me you have a crush on her.”

“What? That is—” Kara nearly nails herself in the forehead with a cupboard. “Alex is…wrong.” A beat. “I still think it’s weird you two text.”

“Really? And here I thought you’d be thrilled. You’re always saying I don’t have enough friends.”

“That is…that is something I say,” Kara admits. “Don’t believe anything Alex says, though. She’s a compulsive liar. And I’m a much better friend than she is, for the record!”

Lena stands up to settle Mike before he starts to whine, casting an amused glance in Kara’s direction. “Kara Danvers, are you jealous of your own sister?”

“No,” Kara says, dropping two spoonfuls of sugar into her coffee. As an afterthought, she adds a third.

“Don’t worry. You’re still my favorite,” Lena mock-whispers, brushing past her as she leaves her empty mug in the sink. “Come on Mike, why don’t you help me pick out an outfit? Let’s give your mommy a morning to relax.”

Kara, dumbfounded, watches Lena go and thinks did that really happen? But then her shock is quickly being replaced by the dreamy idea of being Lena’s favorite, and so she takes Lena’s advice and enjoys her coffee without another thought about Adam.

(That is, until Ms. Grant sends her a text proclaiming that there is urgent CatCo business for Kara to fix.)

Her peaceful morning quickly becomes a frantic one. “Lena, I have to go in early,” Kara calls, forgetfully yanking off her pajama top before she remembers she is hovering in the hallway and really shouldn’t be changing in front of any windows. “Cat just texted me. I’m so sorry, are—are you sure you can still take Mike?”

From beyond her door, Lena shouts, “Yes, we’ll be fine! Go!”

Mike screams unintelligibly too, but in an excited way. Kara takes that to mean he’ll be okay.

With her conscience clear, she manages to catch an earlier bus; she also nearly spills Ms. Grant’s coffee as she rushes upstairs. The elevator is too crowded and she has to take the steps two at a time in her haste, only to find her boss… seated at her desk, already calmly sipping out of a styrofoam coffee cup.

Kara’s mouth falls open in a silent protest. Cat squints up at her as if she’s personally offended her somehow.

“Kira, what are you doing?” Cat says. “You’re all sweaty.”

“I was trying to rush?” Kara tries. She swiftly holds out the coffee cup, and then rethinks the action immediately after when Cat just furrows her brow. “I’m…sorry, Ms. Grant.”

“Now what are you apologizing for?” Cat rests her palms on her desk and rises to her feet, practically amused. “Go on, humor me.”

“I’m, um, apologizing for not being here fast enough?” Kara pushes her glasses up her nose and is dismayed to find out she really is all sweaty.

“Good answer, but you don’t have anything to be sorry for,” Cat says. “Siobhan here has your duties covered.” She nods beyond her office, where Siobhan is already occupying Kara’s desk.

Kara—who hasn’t put aside time to pack up said desk yet—is officially confused. “But you said you needed me.”

“Oh, that’s right. I meant to keep you updated,” Cat says. “The degenerate suing me has dropped the case. I want you to meet with one of the Zorel sisters to find out why.”

“No offense, Ms. Grant, but why do you need the specifics?” Kara asks, slowly. “I’m pretty sure they won’t even be at liberty to say anything…”

Cat sighs, effectively cutting Kara off. “I have no time for your moral speeches, Kira,” she says. “Normally I would not care to follow up on someone’s else error in judgement—you know that. But one of the owners reached out saying she wanted to explain her reasoning. She asked for you specifically.”

“She did?” Kara says. “They remembered me?”

“Well, she really said she’d settle for talking to my assistant, so…I would count that as yes.” Cat makes a shooing motion with her hand; Kara takes a small step backward, but does not leave.

“When did this happen?”

“Siobhan has the details. Go bother her instead,” Cat sighs. “I have to deal with a terrible headache.”

Winn appears at the door, mouth opening to speak, but he’s silenced with another loud sigh.

“Great, two headaches. Come in, Witt. Kyra, close the door after him!”

“That’s a new one,” Winn whispers on his way in, and Kara shrugs.

“I’m immune to them by now.”

Cat’s words don’t truly sink in until Kara is walking away and then Siobhan is in her path. Kara still doesn’t know how to act around her; something about the way Siobhan looks at her leaves Kara inexplicably nervous.

“Hey! Siobhan,” Kara says. The way Siobhan smiles at her name—amused, but casual—nearly has Kara tripping over nothing. “Cat said you had the details about the meeting with Zorel and Law?”

“Yes, I do.” Siobhan peels a sticky note off her monitor and hands it over, still regarding Kara in that curious way. “You know, your shirt is inside out.”

“Uh.” Kara pats down her wrinkled button-up with a sheepish smile. “Right, I knew that.”

To her surprise, Siobhan merely laughs lightly. “I hope you don’t mind that I took over your desk already,” she says. “Cat said you wouldn’t.”

“I don’t mind,” says Kara quickly. “I’m without a desk now, but I’ll manage.”

“I guess we’ll have to share it then.” Siobhan is flirting, right? Kara can honestly say she’s never been able to tell when someone is hitting on her, but this feels too suggestive to not be flirting.

So this is what it feels like, Kara thinks, and the funny thing is she kind of really likes the idea of being flirted with.

.

.

.

It has become slightly easier to have Veronica around so often.

She is clearly making a considerate effort to be nicer—probably at Lena’s request, but still—and when she comes by the apartment, she’s cordial. She even brings by small gifts for Mike sometimes, and a bottle of expensive liquor for Kara if she remembers. (Kara never tells her she doesn’t drink very much, instead opting to give the fancy rum or whiskey to Lena.)

Either way, it’s progress. Kara is getting pretty good at the whole ignoring-her-own-jealousy thing; take now for instance, because Veronica has her arm around Lena as they sit on the couch and Kara doesn’t even bat an eye when she enters the apartment.

“Hi guys,” she says cheerfully. “What’re you watching?”

“That giant squid documentary,” Lena answers offhandedly, and Kara’s grip over jealousy slackens considerably; she had suggested Lena watch that, and they’d planned to see it together. As in, no Veronica included.

But she catches herself on the feeling. “Fun!” she says, as cheerfully as she can muster. “Don’t mind us. We’ll get out of your hair.” She hefts a fussing Mike up in her arms, half-dragging the walker he’s begun to pad around the apartment in with one hand.

“Oh no, stay,” Lena protests. “Mike can walk around the living room more comfortably.”

“I don’t want to interrupt though…”

“By all means,” Veronica interjects. “Stay.”

Kara glances at her skeptically, but sees nothing venomous in her stare. At last she relents; she sets Mike up in his walker, lets him roam as he pleases, and starts unwinding for the day.

She is uncomfortably aware of Veronica’s presence. Lena has shifted so that Veronica is no longer pressed against her, but Veronica doesn’t seem to mind—all she does is cross her legs and continue to observe the scene unfolding before her.

If Kara were jealous—which she isn’t—she might think something petty, like why does Veronica show up to a movie night in heels, but she actively pushes any such thought towards the back of her mind.

“So, um,” Kara says, sitting at the edge of the couch and trying not to look as awkward as she feels. “How was your day? Is Lex still…”

“Leaving me to handle the brunt of his work? Yes,” Lena says, with an eye roll that’s more exasperated than anything. “He’s ridiculous.”

“That sucks.” Kara tries not to look at Veronica and fails. (Veronica seems too amused for Kara’s liking). “You know, I could always talk to him for you. Beat him up if I have to.”

Lena laughs. “That’s not necessary, but I do appreciate the sentiment.” Much to Kara’s dismay, she quirks an eyebrow and adds, “Though I like that aggressive side to you, Kara. Very…unexpected.”

Kara’s cheeks flare hotly. “Well, I’m a complex person,” she tries.

Mike thankfully distracts everyone from their conversation; he shrieks as if to attract their attention, then kicks forward in his wobbly way that’s not quite walking but close.

Lena smiles sweetly at him, the kind of smile that Kara could melt over. “What is it, Mike?” she says. “Do you want to watch with us, hm?” She squeezes his chubby cheek, which makes him beam.

He is such an active baby, curious to a fault—maybe that’s why he tries several times to walk over the rug in the living room, even though his walker refuses to move with him. He gives a frustrated wail, but before Kara can help, Lena steps in instead.

This is exactly why Kara can’t seem to get over Lena. She’s just so kind and loving; it is very hard not to spend all day gazing at her longingly, wondering what it would be like to feel Lena’s lazy day sweatshirt under her fingertips as she kissed her. Or even to know what it would be like to kiss Lena.

(Kara may or may not be terrible at this whole “friends first” mantra she’s been trying to stick to.)

“Have you guys eaten?” Kara asks. “I can make dinner.”

“We ordered in, actually,” Lena replies. “If you want some leftovers, you’re welcome to mine. It’s the mixed veggie carton in the fridge.”

“You ordered the lamest thing,” Kara feigns a groan. This is easy—teasing, sharing leftovers, catching up like friends do. This, Kara is good at. “No potstickers?”

“I owe you them,” Lena promises, and when she playfully swats at Kara’s shoulder it’s almost okay.

Almost, because Veronica is still there. She clears her throat quite obviously and says, “I think I’m going to go.”

“Already?” Lena is immediately concerned. “I thought you weren’t busy tonight.”

Kara decides she shouldn’t be listening to this conversation; she investigates the kitchen instead, not-so-subtly bringing Mike and his walker with her.

Sometimes (and this may be the jealousy, but can just as easily be simple curiosity) she wonders about Lena and Veronica’s relationship. Veronica has never stayed the night, or ever been suggested to stay the night. Kara chalks it up to Mike’s presence in the apartment, but at times it feels like Lena is much more reserved with Veronica than Kara expected her to be.

Not that Kara notices, or anything…because they’re only friends, and that would be. Wrong, probably.

She yanks open the fridge and puts the thought out of her mind. “Next time, you have to tell Lena to order potstickers for me,” she tells Mike. “And let her know I’ll pay her back.”

All Mike does is spit up. Figures.

.

.

.

The thing about trying to get over your sort-of-maybe best friend is that some stupidity should be involved. Like, as a general rule.

Kara honestly would be doing a disservice to the getting-over-best-friend community (she’s positive it’s a thing) if she didn’t try to help Lena out before finally dropping the idea of a romance with her. It’s not that getting over her means not caring about her as a friend, exactly; she’ll still care about Lena even when those pesky romantic feelings go away. But still. It would be a nice gesture to help Lena anyway.

Which is why Kara is standing in Luthor Corp with her head held high as she asks, “Can you get me a meeting with Lex?”

Silence. Then, Jess levels Kara with the most unimpressed stare she has ever seen. “I don’t work for Lex, I work for Lena,” she deadpans. “Do you have any other useless requests for me, or can I keep working?”

“Jess,” Kara pleads. “I need to talk to him.”

“Then ask Lena to get you a meeting with him, not me,” Jess says. “She’d get you an interview in a heartbeat.”

“But that’s the thing,” says Kara quickly. “Lena can’t know I want to meet Lex.”

“What?”

“She can’t. Come on, aren’t you worried for her too?” Kara lowers her voice. “Lex keeps piling work on her and she’s struggling. I want to ask him what’s going on.”

“Lena doesn’t need a knight in shining armor, Kara,” Jess says stubbornly, but her resolve is weakening—Kara can tell. “She would’ve talked to Lex herself if the workload was too great.”

“You know Lena better than anyone,” Kara counters, “and we both know that’s not true.” When Jess says nothing, Kara lifts Mike up and says, “Please? Do it for Lena. And if not for her, then for Mike! You can’t say no to this cute face.”

Jess narrows her eyes. “Fine,” she says sourly. “I’ll see what I can do.”

“Thank you! Mike, say thank you to Jess.”

“Eee.”

“That’s thank you in baby,” Kara says.

The expression on Jess’s face remains nothing but unimpressed, but she softens just enough to give Mike a little smile. “You know,” she says, “you’re not supposed to be on this floor.”

Kara shrugs. “Yeah, I know. But we had to break a few rules for the better good.”

“No, seriously. Did security just let you up?”

“That’s between me and security.”

.

.

.

“Alex, I don’t know what I want to do.”

“Work, hopefully,” Alex says, then unhelpfully tosses a piece of popcorn at Kara’s forehead. “Sit up, just looking at you makes me dizzy.”

Kara dutifully sits up on the couch; she’s unbothered by hanging off the cushion, but apparently Alex is feeling traitorous today. “You've got to help me,” she implores. “I have no idea what to do.”

“We’ve established that, yes,” Alex says.

“Alex.

“What do you want me to say, Kara? I love you and all, but I don’t know shit about your job.” Alex mercifully pops her next piece of popcorn into her mouth. “Why don't you ask around the office? See what jobs are available?”

“Do you think…” Kara starts, then rethinks it immediately. “No, it’s silly.”

“What?”

“It’s—dumb.” Kara shakes her head. “I could never, so…”

“What?”

Kara hesitates. “Well, Lena said I could be a good reporter. Do you think she’s right?”

Alex squints at her. “Lena said that, huh?”

“Yeah…”

“That’s gay.”

“She was being supportive of me!” Kara immediately snatches a pillow off her side of the couch to nail Alex with.

Alex yelps, cradling her popcorn bowl close to her chest. “Hey! I’m just saying, that sounds pretty gay to me.”

“I’m serious, Alex. Do you think I should try?” Kara asks.

“I think it’s not a bad idea,” Alex shrugs, “provided my opinion matters as much as Lena’s.”

Kara smacks her with the pillow again for good measure, but she’s relieved all the same. “Really? You think so?”

“Why not? You’re annoyingly persistent, friendly, all that jazz.” Alex takes a handful of popcorn and holds it out like a peace offering. “You could really make a difference.”

“I’m not sure if I would be good at it,” Kara admits. “What if I’m terrible? Then I’ll have wasted my chance at a career change.”

“Well, do you want to do a terrible job?”

“No! What kind of question is that?”

“A necessary one, dummy,” Alex snorts. “If you go into this thinking you’re going to do an awful job then you will. But if you give it your all, you’ll kill it. I know reverse psychology.”

Kara considers this advice for a moment. “I don’t think you know what reverse psychology is.”

“Fuck off, I skimmed the syllabus for that psychology course once upon a time.” Alex hands over the nearly-emptied popcorn bowl. “Do you have any ice cream?”

“Wait, why are you eating all my food? I should be the one stress-eating here!” Kara defensively hugs the bowl. “I’m having a crisis.”

“You have a crisis every other week,” Alex says, “so I need payment to keep up with you.”

“Hey, who was the one who listened to your entire spiel about Maggie for three days straight—”

“You’re my little sister. It’s part of the job description,” Alex calls as she makes her way into the kitchen. “You want rocky road or key lime pie?”

“Rocky road, key lime is Lena’s favorite.”

“Lena eats ice cream?” Alex says, re-emerging with the carton and two spoons. “What a liar. She tells me she eats healthy when you’re around.”

“She’s definitely lying—I’m a terrible influence on her diet,” Kara says, accepting the bigger spoon and nearly inhaling her first bite.

Alex shakes her head disapprovingly. “I knew it,” she says. “Please tell me you two don’t feed Mike nothing but garbage.”

“Mike eats a very well-balanced array of baby foods,” Kara protests. “I’m a great influence there! His favorite is the carrots. Mine is the squash, but we both aren’t a big fan of peas.”

“Do…do you just,” Alex pauses, “eat his baby food with him?”

“I have to try each kind we buy,” Kara says. “What if it’s gross? I can’t feed my son gross food.”

“It’s baby food. I—” Alex stops, then shakes her head once more. “Promise me you will never, ever get a dog.”

“Hey! That is an unfair comparison.”

Alex steals the carton back. “Kara, you know I’m your number one supporter, right?”

Kara readjusts her position on the couch so she’s seated cross-legged across from Alex, takes in how suddenly serious she is. “Of course I do,” she replies.

“Then take the reporting job. You’re going to be awesome at it,” Alex says. “I believe in you.”

Kara smiles. “Really?”

“Really.”

“You’re the best sister,” Kara decides. “Ever.”

“I know,” Alex says, licking her melting spoonful of ice cream smugly. “And as your best and older sister, I’m obligated to tell you to stop tasting your son’s baby food, because that’s not something people do.”

“But how am I supposed to judge if he’ll like it?”

“This is not open for discussion, okay, I’m just leaving it out there. It’s weird.”

.

.

.

 

“Mr. Luthor?”

For a moment Kara thinks he hasn’t heard; she takes in a deep breath, repeats herself, but when she opens her eyes she sees Lex is tilting his head wordlessly in the direction of his office.

“Ms. Danvers, what a surprise,” Lex says, taking out a pen from his mouth as he walks briskly by Kara down the hallway. “What brings you by Luthor Corp? Lena’s in Metropolis on business.”

“I know.” Kara hurries after him, barely able to keep up with his pace. “I actually wanted an audience. With you,” she tacks on hastily. “Just the two of us.”

“For a dear friend of my sister’s? I’d be delighted,” Lex says. “Sit, sit! Can I get you anything to drink? Coffee? Tea? Water?”

“No, thank you.” Kara takes a tentative seat on the couch, but Lex never slows. He continues to walk about the room, opening his briefcase and immediately losing its contents.

“So what’s this about?” Lex hums. He’s ignored Kara’s polite decline of anything to drink, because he hands her a glass of chilled iced tea next. “Are you planning some kind of surprise for Lena? She never quits talking about you. It’s great that you two are such good friends.”

Kara blinks. “Yeah, she’s…great, obviously. But this isn’t about any surprise for her.”

“That’s a pity. I love a good party,” says Lex jovially. “What brings you by, then?”

Now that she is face-to-face with the eccentric and elusive Lex Luthor, Kara isn’t proud to admit she falters. “Um,” she says. “I wanted to ask—about your role in Luthor Corp.”

Lex makes a show of patting down his pockets. “Oh? Are we on the record?” he says. “Cat sure knows how to throw a curveball. Does she have her assistants shaking down all the CEOs?”

“I'm not her assistant anymore,” Kara interjects. “And I’m not here on CatCo business either.”

“But you are here on business.” Lex presses his fingertips together, delighted. “What can I do for you, Ms. Danvers?”

“Well…I was hoping to ask about your role, as CEO,” Kara says. “On a personal note.”

“A personal note?” Lex raises an eyebrow. “Go on.”

Kara sighs. “I don’t know how else to ask this,” she admits. “But why are you piling so much work on Lena? She doesn’t complain about it, but she’s…she’s a hard worker. And when you expect so much of her when you’re supposed to be the one in charge, it wears her down.”

“Ah,” Lex muses, “you’re afraid I’m abusing my power.”

A hard-hitting journalist might’ve brought that up, but Kara’s novice enough that she merely blinks and says, “I’m actually just worried about Lena.”

Lex stares at her in a way Kara can’t comprehend—a bit proud, a bit surprised, a bit bit perplexed. “It’s great that you care about her,” he says. “That’s a very good trait in a friend. Loyalty, that is. You don’t see it in many people.”

“Uh. Thanks?”

“Believe me, it’s a compliment,” Lex says. “It’s admirable that you’ve come to fight on her behalf. I wonder, did she send you? That wouldn’t be her style.”

“No, she has no idea I’m here,” Kara says. “I know I’m stepping way out of line by talking to you, but I don’t understand what’s going on.”

“I have my reasons, Ms. Danvers. But rest assured, I will take your complaints into mind,” Lex chuckles. “Don’t worry about my sister. She’s going to be well taken care of.”

“Right, except—”

“And trust me on this,” Lex says. “She will not take kindly to you fighting her battles. So how about we keep this conversation between us?”

“I don’t mean to overstep,” Kara says. “Or fight her battles. Lena’s—she’s the strongest person I know, okay? I just want to understand.”

“Then you should go, before you try to start that fight,” Lex suggests. He has a peculiar approving expression on his face; he is not angry, not upset, not taken aback. He looks…glad. “But hey, we should have dinner sometime! All three of us. Lena’s always mentioning you have a fondness for Chinese—I know a place.”

Kara can understand this as a dismissal. “Sure,” she says, without meaning it. “Thanks for meeting with me.”

“Believe me, the pleasure was all mine.” Lex shoots to his feet to shake her hand, wide grin never faltering. “You’re quite a force, Ms. Danvers. I can see why my sister is so fond of you.”

“Yeah,” Kara struggles to find a suitable response, “thanks, I like her too.”

She leaves without a goodbye (or a clue of what just happened).

.

.

.

“How did Alex handle living with you for so long? I’m beginning to think it’s too much for me.”

“That is mean and uncalled for, Lena.” Kara pouts. “If you want me to put back the brownies all you have to do is ask.”

“Kara, my darling roommate,” a lengthy pause worthy of Lena’s dramatics, “will you please put back at least two boxes of brownies? There is no way we can eat all of those.”

“For you, I’ll do it,” Kara sighs, placing three boxes back onto the shelves. “But I think I could’ve eaten them all myself.”

“I don’t doubt that, and I’m severely afraid of what you put your stomach through.” Lena picks a loaf of wheat bread to add to their cart, and Kara eyes it skeptically but says nothing. “It’s nice that we can do this.”

“What, bicker about store brand brownie mix?”

“No,” Lena snorts. “I mean go grocery shopping together. You agree, right Mike?”

At the sound of his name, the baby perks up. “Hmhm,” he babbles.

Lena beams at him as though he’s said something sage. “Yes, exactly.”

“We always go grocery shopping together,” Kara points out. “Or we used to.”

“Key word used,” Lena says. “I’ve missed you, you know? Even though we live together it seems like I don’t see much of you these days.” She takes over to push the cart, giving Kara the chance to get on her tiptoes and reach for the cereal. “Though I’m sure that’s because you’re a hard-hitting reporter now.”

“Hardly,” Kara scoffs. “My new boss is determined to make sure I never step foot out of the office. I think he hates me.”

Lena frowns. “How can anyone hate you? Alex once described you as ‘sunshine personified,’ and I’m inclined to agree with her assessment,” she says.

“Alex said that because she’s mean,” Kara stresses, “and wants to give me dumb nicknames.” A pause. “But, I don’t know. Snapper seems really grumpy. Nothing I do will make him warm up to me! He won’t even give me a chair.”

“Is that…a sanctioned professional decision?”

Kara shrugs. “It’s his domain,” she says.

“Still,” Lena says. “Maybe if you talked to Cat…”

“I don’t want to bother her. Besides, I’ll look like I’m trying to abuse my position,” Kara says. “Running to Cat like she’s supposed to fight my battles for me won’t win Snapper over. He’s going to think just because Cat is my son’s grandmother—” She frowns. “Wow, that still doesn’t sound right.”

“Do you want me to talk to him? I can be very scary if need be,” Lena offers.

“You? Scary?” Kara squints at Lena playfully. “As if. Intimidating? Maybe sometimes. But scary? No way.”

Lena rolls her eyes. “You’ve corrupted me,” she says. “I used to be this cold-hearted, untouchable person, and now I’m standing in the cereal aisle of Walmart in sweatpants.”

“Don’t lie,” Kara says. “I know you’re a big softie. Who else would give up their Halloween plans to carry a baby’s plastic pumpkin around all night?”

“Someone with too much time on her hands, clearly,” Lena deadpans, but there’s something about the way she looks away that’s puzzling—something about the way she blushes, like she is remembering something she shouldn’t. “Come on, we can’t stand here all night. We need milk.”

“Ik,” Mike trills in agreement. “Eeeyak.”

“Alright, I’m outnumbered,” Kara surrenders. “Just as long as it’s not skim milk again.”

“What’s wrong with skim milk? You still drank it,” Lena says.

“Because you tricked me!”

Lena nudges Kara out of the way so she can continue pushing the cart. “I’m sorry, is it supposed to be my fault you don’t read the labels on your food?” she calls over her shoulder. “I don’t recall that being part of the lease.”

Kara trails after her, discreetly picking up one of the brownie boxes on her way out. “Sometimes I’m in a rush,” she says defensively. “And I blindly trust the milk gallon.”

“There’s your first mistake.” Lena reaches into the cooler and hands off the milk gallon to Kara, raising an eyebrow to the brownie box tucked under Kara’s arm but saying nothing of it. “What’s next on the list?”

“Um…cake,” Kara reads. “Wait, cake? Did I sneak this on here and forget about it?”

“Fuck,” Lena says, suddenly looking less than pleased. “No, that was me. I’m supposed to bring cake to some office party Lex is throwing. I don’t think Walmart cake is what he had in mind, though.”

“All cake is good cake,” Kara offers. “Plus, Walmart cakes have awesome icing.”

“Their icing looks radioactive,” Lena says. “I’ll have to put a reminder in my phone. Seeing how I bought a bakery, it would be outlandish to get a cake done anywhere but there.” Before she has a chance to take her phone out of her purse, it buzzes with an incoming call. “Hold on, this might be…oh, God, I have to take this. Wait for me by the freezer aisle?”

“Yeah, of course,” Kara says. Her voice tapers off; Lena is already walking away.

Mike raises his arms out once Kara leans against the shopping cart. Kara knows she shouldn’t encourage his restlessness because they still have more shopping to do, but she lifts him out anyway. He immediately tries to lead her over to a large display of Kinder eggs; he loves their bright packaging, and won’t let up until Kara places one in his hands.

Kara lets him play with it, but keeps a close eye on him to make sure he doesn’t try to shove it in his mouth. Then, with her son on her hip, she reads off the next item on the grocery list and makes her way towards the frozen pizzas.

When Lena catches up with them again, she has a pensive frown in place and a box of granola bars under her arm. “Sorry about that,” she mutters distastefully. “What’s next?”

“Is everything okay?” Kara hedges.

Lena nods before Kara even finishes her question. “Yes, don’t worry,” she says. “I just had to handle a work call. I might have to rush our shopping trip, if that’s alright?”

“Hey, no, it’s fine,” Kara assures her. “We’ll just have to live for the next few weeks without frozen pizza and…dog food. At least, I think that’s what it says here. I can’t read your handwriting.”

“Oh, shut up,” Lena says, but her face softens. “Are you sure you’re okay with this?”

“Yes,” Kara stresses. “Besides—I added half of the remaining items when you weren’t looking anyway.”

Lena shakes her head in mock-exasperation, but she’s smiling. “I already knew that,” she says.

“Only because I told you.”

“No, I’m pretty sure the fact that I don’t eat frozen pizza gave it away,” Lena says. “But you know we don’t have to share a grocery list. If you want frozen pizza, you don’t have to run it past me.”

“I know, but it makes sense,” Kara says. “We’re roommates. All three of us!” She holds out her hand to Mike and says, “Right Mike? High five.”

He seriously considers it for a second, but ultimately only leans forward to bump his forehead against Kara’s palm. Kara laughs, then drops a kiss to the top of his head and decides they’ll work on that.

When she lifts her head up again Lena is watching, eyes as soft as her expression. “I’m lucky to have you two as roommates,” she says, and she reaches over to squeeze Kara’s forearm gently. “Thank you for understanding.”

Kara swallows. “Of course,” she says, voice coming out a little quieter than expected.

Lena lets go of her hand after a beat, almost as if just remembering where they are. “Grab your pizzas,” she says hastily. “I’ll go get paper towels, and we can be on our way.”

“Right, yeah,” Kara says lamely. She files this day as a failure in the bigger picture of getting over Lena—but in a way, it’s something of a win. Lena thinks she’s lucky to have Kara and Mike as roommates.

Kara couldn’t stop marveling at the idea if she tried.

.

.

.

Somehow they have fallen into a pattern of domesticity that Kara never knew could exist.

Lena sits at the kitchen table studying paperwork with a heavy, furrowed brow while Kara feeds Mike across from her and tries not to comment on how stressed Lena looks. Mike is unable to catch on to the tension, kicking his legs and babbling a mile a minute between spoonfuls.

“Lena?” Kara ventures to say. “We’re not bothering you, are we?”

“Hm?” Lena raises her cheek off her fist, the proof of her discomfort a resulting pink imprint. “No, why would you think that?”

“You have that angry look. Like, the one you make whenever Winn is taking too long to roll the dice for Monopoly.”

Lena smiles, a faded, tired smile that makes Kara’s heart twist in her chest. “Well, these old men are just as frustrating as Winn playing Monopoly,” she says. “You two are definitely not.”

“Okay, if you’re sure…because I can take Mike into the living room instead if you want—” Kara tries.

“No, he’s not doing anything wrong,” Lena says. She reaches over to nudge Mike’s chin, completely ignoring the fact that she gets pureed carrots on her finger. “In fact, he’s cheering me up already.”

Mike squirms, pleased, and bangs his hands against his tray. He raises his sticky arms up, waiting for Lena to carry him, but when Kara offers him another spoonful he decides he’d rather stay where he is.

“What has Lex left you to do tonight?” Kara asks. “Do you—I mean, is there anything I can help you with?”

“Lex has decided he needs our revenue data by midnight,” Lena mutters. “I know it’s my job. I handle numbers, he handles the big stuff. But I’ve been so busy doing his work that I’ve been neglecting my own.”

“That’s not right, Lena,” Kara says, feeling her own brow crinkle. “He shouldn’t expect so much from you.”

“Well, he won’t listen to me when I tell him he needs to manage his time better. You know, he is just so irresponsible lately, I don’t know what it is. Maybe he’s finally lost his damn—” Lena pauses, casts an uneasy glance at Mike. “I shouldn’t talk about him like that. Forgive me, I’m ranting.”

“You can rant all you want,” Kara assures her. “No judgement.”

“Thank you, Kara, but it’s really not that bad,” Lena sighs. “I’m sorry for being so…disheveled, lately. I just don’t know if I can handle more of this.”

“You don’t have to apologize for anything,” Kara insists. “Lex is the one who needs to apologize to you. He’s a—a—a flake. Yeah, that’s the word.”

Before Lena can reply, her phone goes off. “That’ll be him,” she says. “Be on standby, I may take you up on the offer to beat him up.” This she says with a fond, joking wink, but Kara still waits for the outcome anyway.

Lena disappears into her room ten minutes into the call; Mike finishes his dinner in the next five, and still nothing. Lena doesn’t come out of her room for movie night, but Kara puts one on TV anyway—she has no idea what it’s about, only knows that Mike dozes off somewhere in the first half hour.

Their front door opens around nine p.m. Kara has put Mike to bed by then, and has also started up the sequel to the forgettable film she’d picked; the sound of a key in the lock startles her enough that she falls off the couch.

Veronica squints at her. “Kara?” she says, closing her fist around the spare apartment key.

“Veronica?” Kara haphazardly throws her glasses on. Then, as an afterthought, she props herself up into a sitting position. “What are you doing here?” What she really means is since when do you have a spare key, and why didn’t Lena tell me about it, but she doesn’t ask.

“Lena called me,” Veronica says. “Is she here?”

“She’s in her room.” Kara pretends to stretch her arms overhead, but really scrambles to pick herself up off the floor. “She called you? She’s done talking to Lex?”

“Yes…” Veronica is eyeing her strangely, like there’s something Kara doesn’t know yet. “Can I sit?”

“Uh.” Kara scoots over and lamely reaches up to pat the couch. “Yeah, of course.”

Veronica tentatively takes a seat. “I take it she didn’t tell you I’d be coming?”

“Nope.” Kara hugs her knees to her chest and pretends she has chosen this spot on the floor after all. “So. This weather. Really something, huh?”

Lena saves them both from awkward small talk when she finally emerges out of her bedroom (it’s just as well; Veronica had looked unsure of what to reply to Kara’s question).

“Oh, Veronica, you’re here,” Lena says. “Thank you for coming on such short notice. Kara—I hope it’s alright that I invited her over.”

“Yeah, definitely,” Kara assures her hastily. “It’s your apartment.”

“It’s our apartment,” Lena corrects absentmindedly. In fact, the expression on her face remains rather absentminded as well—almost as if she’s in a daze. “I wanted to tell you both the news.”

Kara rests her chin on her right knee and bites back a question of what this is all about. Veronica leans forward slightly, concerned in a way Kara has never witnessed, because Lena begins to pace.

It takes another minute before Lena finally stops. “Lex told me that he’s taking a leave from the company,” she announces unceremoniously. “A permanent leave.” She pauses. “Apparently, he’s been steadily increasing my workload to take on his, just to see if I can handle taking over the position as CEO. It’s so typical of him to make this decision without telling me about it first, I—” Another pause, this time lengthier. “Sorry. I’m…still processing.”

“How do you feel right now?” Kara broaches carefully. “You look kind of. Um. Upset?”

“I’m not upset,” Lena counters. “At least, I don’t think I am. I’m a little annoyed that he took such a dramatic route, but…” She trails off. “I don’t think I should take the job.”

“But did he offer the job,” Veronica speaks up suddenly, “or did he just give it to you?”

Lena furrows her brow. “What do you mean?”

“Do you have a choice about whether you get to take this job or not?” Veronica asks. “If I know your brother, I’d assume it’s the latter.”

“But he can’t do that,” Kara says. “Right? He should…he should prep you, or—or something. He can’t just dump this responsibility on you, can he?”

“He’s the boss,” Lena sighs. “I—I guess you’re right, Veronica. He never quite specified if this was optional.” She sinks down onto the couch, clasping her hands before her tightly; Kara can see her knuckles go white. “I’ve basically been the CEO for some time now, if you think about it.”

“You can still say no,” Kara tries, but Lena shakes her head.

“No, it’d be foolish to even consider doing that,” Lena mutters. “Of course I’ll be taking the job—it’s Luthor Corp, after all. It’s expected of me.”

Veronica reaches over to place her hand over Lena’s. “Maybe you need to sleep on it,” she says. “To let this sink in. Kara may be right…it’s a big task to take on, especially if it’s unplanned.”

Kara doesn’t know if she should be offended or not, but she pushes the thought aside; this is about Lena right now. She rises from her spot on the floor to join the others on the couch, albeit hesitantly—she isn’t sure if it’s her place.

But to her surprise, Lena immediately makes space for her. In fact, the instant Kara sits down Lena is moving her hands away from Veronica’s to grasp at Kara’s, allowing their newly-joined hands fall down to her thigh.

“I’m sorry you have to see me like this,” Lena says. It takes Kara a moment to realize she’s speaking to Veronica—Veronica, who eyes Kara and Lena’s joined hands but says nothing about it. “I know I’m overreacting.”

“You don’t have to apologize to me, Lena,” Veronica negates. “I’m glad to be here with you.”

Suddenly it feels like Kara is intruding. She makes a hasty excuse that she needs to check up on Mike, and Lena lets go of her hands; lets go rather swiftly, almost as though she’d forgotten she was still holding on to her.

Baby steps, Kara thinks, that’s how she’s going to get over these feelings. Just one day at a time.
